#5120. Sam Altman Says It's 'Fucking Annoying' His RAM Comments Were Taken Out of Context

Sam Altman Says It's 'Fucking Annoying' His RAM Comments Were Taken Out of Context

OpenAI CEO Sam Altman has hit back at criticism of his comments about computer memory, saying they were taken out of context and that he has long argued for the world to make more of it.

"I actually do want the world to have tons of memory and people to put as much as they want in their computers, and it is fucking annoying that this was taken out of context," Altman tweeted early Wednesday. "I have been arguing for the world to ramp up memory production for a long time."

but only because...i actually do want the world to have tons of memory and people to put as much as they want in their computers, and it is fucking annoying that this was taken out of context.

i have been arguing for the world to ramp up memory production for a long time.

The post came as Altman leaned into the backlash. Replying to users joking about his remarks, he said he "lol'd" at one meme and, when another asked him to rename his X account "Ram Altman," replied "ok yeah," briefly changing his display name before explaining he had only done so because he wanted people to have plenty of memory.

Altman's original comments came in an interview with YouTube channel Cheeky Pint, where he discussed shortages of memory and other components caused by the AI boom. "From like a global allocation perspective, actually, it's quite terrible to have everybody have a bunch of the world's limited supply of DRAM sitting in their own computer and being used 5% of the time," he said. "You want maximum utilization in a cloud if you're trying to deal with this very supply-constrained environment."

In the same interview, Altman also blamed memory makers for being slow to expand production, and said he would be "very sad" if prices for chips and other components did not fall.

The remarks drew widespread criticism, including from Elon Musk, who mocked Altman on Sunday with a meme of the Inglourious Basterds character Hans Landa asking, "You are withholding DRAM from the cloud, are you not?" "The people deserve to have their RAM too!" Musk wrote.

The spat comes amid a global memory shortage driven by demand from AI data centers. SK hynix, one of the world's largest memory makers, warned last week that demand for memory would outstrip supply through 2030.

Contract prices for 16GB DDR5 memory chips rose from $8.70 in October 2025 to $48 in September 2026, an increase of more than 550%, according to Tom's Hardware. On an earnings call this week, Chen Ching-wen, CEO of memory maker Team Group, said there was "no downward price trend whatsoever," and warned that some buyers could face a situation where "even money can't buy goods."

The crunch has already pushed up prices for consumer devices. In April, Meta raised the price of its Quest 3 and Quest 3S headsets by $50 to $100, weeks after Sony increased PlayStation 5 prices.

#5117. Samsung Wallet launches USDC accounts on Solana for US Galaxy users

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Samsung Wallet's Stablecoin Account lets eligible U.S. Galaxy users buy, hold, send and receive USDC on Solana via the app.

Samsung's Stablecoin Account is now live in Samsung Wallet, allowing eligible Galaxy users in the United States to buy, hold and transfer USDC on Solana, according to a statement released Thursday.

Samsung first announced the feature on Oct. 7, with the launch originally expected in the last week of that month.

Around 82 million Galaxy devices in the U.S. are compatible with the service, according to the release. Users must be at least 18 years old, have a Samsung Account and own a compatible device running Android 13 or later. New York residents are excluded.

"For a decade, stablecoins have proved the technology works," Solana Foundation President Lily Liu said. "What they lacked was a way to reach the mainstream."

Users can send USDC to another Samsung Wallet user or a compatible external crypto wallet without paying a Samsung transfer fee. Eligible users can also send money to qualifying bank accounts in more than 60 countries, where recipients may receive local currency, with fees varying by destination.

Licensed stablecoin custodian and infrastructure provider Bastion handles the stablecoin accounts and transfer infrastructure, while Coinbase holds the USDC as its sub-custodian through Coinbase Prime Vault.

Coinbase was already working with Samsung before this launch. In October 2025, Samsung expanded its Coinbase partnership to let U.S. Galaxy users fund Coinbase accounts with Samsung Pay.

Samsung's new Stablecoin Account feature lets users access USDC without installing a separate crypto app or managing private keys themselves. The firm said it also expects to explore online and in-store stablecoin payments, and expansion to additional markets, subject to local regulation.

"Stablecoins have the potential to make moving money around the world faster and easier, and we want Galaxy users to be able to take advantage of that without having to navigate the complexity of traditional crypto tools," said Woncheol Chai, EVP and Head of the Digital Wallet Team at the Mobile eXperience (MX) Business at Samsung Electronics.

#5115. South Korea Pushes 22% Crypto Tax to 2029 in Fourth Delay, Offers Three-Year Loss Carryforward

South Korea's government will ask lawmakers to delay its 22% crypto gains tax to 2029 and let investors carry losses forward for up to three years.

South Korea’s government plans to push back its tax on cryptocurrency gains by another two years to 2029, Finance and Economy Minister Lee Hyeong-il said Thursday, in what would be the fourth delay since the levy was written into law.

The tax is scheduled to take effect in January. To postpone it, the National Assembly must amend the Income Tax Act before then, and Lee said the government would seek lawmakers’ approval during this year’s regular session.

“It weighs heavily on me to propose another delay after the tax has already been postponed three times,” Lee said at a press briefing in Seoul.

Korea legislated the tax in 2020 for a 2022 start. It was then pushed to 2023, 2025 and 2027 as officials conceded that tax systems and investor protections were not ready.

The main reason for the new delay is data on overseas trading. When investors move tokens bought abroad onto a Korean exchange, the exchange may have no record of the purchase price needed to calculate a taxable gain.

That gap closes in 2029, when Korea and the United States begin exchanging information under the OECD-backed Crypto-Asset Reporting Framework, or CARF. Introducing the tax before then could put Korea’s domestic crypto market at a disadvantage, the government said.

“Once we begin receiving cryptocurrency transaction data from the United States in 2029, we will have enough information to tax investors who use US exchanges on the same basis as those who trade in the domestic market,” Lee said.

The government also cited the need to align the tax with the proposed Digital Asset Act, which would set rules for digital asset businesses and strengthen investor protection. Korea’s broader crypto rulebook has been moving on several fronts, including a Bank of Korea push to write stablecoins into foreign exchange law.

Exchanges, investors and lawmakers had pressed for more time, with exchanges saying unclear reporting requirements held up system development and testing.

The rate itself is not changing. Individuals would pay 22%, including local income tax, on annual net income above 2.5 million won, or about $1,869, from selling or lending virtual assets such as bitcoin.

What is new is a proposed three-year loss carryforward. Under current law, only losses from the same year can offset gains, so an investor could owe tax in a profitable year even after losing more the year before.

Under the proposal, losses realized in 2029 could offset gains through 2032. Losses realized before the tax takes effect would not be eligible, the government said, citing the volatility of crypto prices as the reason for the change.

The carryforward would follow similar practices in the U.S. and Britain, the finance ministry said. It also said it will work with the Financial Services Commission to speed up the digital asset law.

The government plans to use the extra two years to collect overseas trading records and analyze them alongside domestic tax data. A working group of industry representatives, experts and government officials will review tax rules for different types of transactions and ways to simplify filing through the first half of 2027.

If the review finds that more legislation is needed, the government plans to submit amendments during the National Assembly’s 2027 regular session. Detailed tax guidelines are due in the first half of 2028.

For Korea’s large base of retail crypto traders, the announcement extends a tax-free window that was supposed to close in 2022. Whether 2029 holds depends on the Assembly passing the amendment in the coming weeks and on the U.S. data exchange starting on schedule.

#5113. Paraguay president resigns after collapse of Ueno digital bank

Santiago Peña stepped down amid mounting scrutiny over his former ties to Ueno Bank’s parent company, as the lender’s collapse left millions of customers unable to access their funds.

Update (Nov. 5, 21:21 UTC): This article has been updated to include comments from X4T founder and CEO David Eisenmann and Tether CEO Paolo Ardoino.

Paraguayan President Santiago Peña announced his resignation Thursday amid a deepening political crisis triggered by the collapse of digital lender Ueno Bank.

In a video posted to X, Peña said he was stepping down to preserve the country’s stability, social peace and democratic institutions. He added that his priority was protecting Paraguayans who had entrusted their savings to the financial system.

Paraguay’s central bank announced resolution proceedings for Ueno Bank on Tuesday after finding that its solvency ratio had fallen below half the 12% regulatory minimum.

Peña faced mounting political scrutiny over his former business ties to Grupo Vázquez, the conglomerate that controls Ueno Bank. He remained a business partner of the group until March 2025, according to AP.

Peña has denied any wrongdoing, arguing that the controversy began as a regulatory matter before escalating into a political dispute.

The banking crisis also coincided with elevated asking prices for Tether’s USDt stablecoin on Bybit’s Paraguay peer-to-peer marketplace. One listing viewed early Thursday offered USDt at 6,942 Paraguayan guaraníes, approximately 13% above the central bank’s Nov. 4 reference rate of 6,144 guaraníes per US dollar.

His resignation came as lawmakers debated impeachment proceedings over alleged conflicts of interest, misuse of pension funds and institutional cover-ups linked to Ueno Bank’s collapse, according to Reuters.

Peña, who was elected in April 2023 and took office that August, was serving a five-year term scheduled to end in 2028. Under Paraguay’s Constitution, Vice President Pedro Alliana would succeed him for the remainder of the term if Congress accepts his resignation.

Ueno Bank had grown rapidly since 2021 into one of Paraguay’s largest financial institutions, serving more than 3 million customers in a country of roughly 6.5 million people.

Ueno first came under regulatory scrutiny in June over loans to companies linked to its controlling shareholder, Grupo Vázquez. In an October statement, the bank acknowledged receiving regulatory notices on June 5 and July 24 requiring adjustments to its related-party lending exposure.

On Nov. 2, the Central Bank of Paraguay initiated resolution proceedings after adjustments to the bank’s financial statements pushed its solvency ratio down to 5.56%, less than half of the 12% regulatory minimum. Authorities suspended the bank’s operations, restricting customers’ access to their deposits.

David Eisenmann, founder and CEO of Paraguayan crypto exchange X4T, told Cointelegraph that customers are turning to crypto to pay bills and protect their savings amid the banking crisis. Some are cashing out their holdings into guaraníes or US dollars to pay salaries and settle debts, while others are buying dollar-backed stablecoins.

“Customers are turning to their crypto holdings and converting them into guaraníes or dollars to make payments they couldn’t make with their bank deposits,” he said. “When that access is restricted, people look for other sources of liquidity.”

On X4T, USDT and USDC prices in guaraníes have risen about 8% in recent weeks, Eisenmann said, reflecting the dollar’s gains against the local currency. That recovery began in October and accelerated this week, so the increase could not be attributed entirely to Ueno Bank’s collapse, he added.

The dollar gained about 1.6% against the guaraní between Oct. 1 and the month’s final trading day, central bank data shows. Despite its rally this week, it remained about 2.8% below in 2026.

Tether CEO Paolo Ardoino also weighed in, writing on X that “USDt is a lifeline for hundreds millions of people in the world,” in response to Cointelegraph’s coverage of the banking turmoil.

On Thursday, Paraguay’s central bank announced that it would start deposit guarantee payments to eligible clients with balances of up to 10 million guaraníes (about $1,630), provided they had no outstanding debts to Ueno and held accounts at other financial institutions.

Under the country’s deposit insurance system, deposits are protected up to 228.3 million guaraníes (about $37,200) per depositor, per financial institution.

The bank’s collapse sparked protests in the nation’s capital, Asunción, where hundreds of depositors, pensioners and workers demanded the return of their savings and accountability from government officials.

Paraguayan authorities detained eight Ueno Bank executives on Nov. 3, including Grupo Vázquez president Miguel Vázquez and bank president Juan Manuel Gustale, according to local newspaper ABC Color. A court subsequently ordered six executives held in pretrial detention and placed two others under house arrest.

#5111. Bitcoin Erases Election Rally as AI Stocks Plunge, $428 Million in Crypto Longs Get Wiped Out

Bitcoin Erases Election Rally as AI Stocks Plunge, $428 Million in Crypto Longs Get Wiped Out

Bitcoin erased Wednesday's post-election rebound Thursday as a technology stock selloff spread to cryptocurrencies, triggering hundreds of millions in forced liquidations.

Bitcoin fell to about $76,500, down nearly 5% and back near levels seen before Wednesday's jump above $80,000. Ethereum shed more than 5%, while other major tokens suffered comparable losses. Roughly 90% of the top 100 cryptocurrencies were in the red during the selloff, while roughly $428 million in crypto longs were liquidated in one hour.

Wall Street offered no shelter. The S&P 500 lost about 2.6% in late-morning trading, while the Nasdaq sank nearly 4%, approaching a correction, or a 10% drop from its peak.

The election is part of the answer, but anxiety over AI spending and interest rates is making the damage worse.

Democrats' sweep of Congress initially rattled investors expecting tougher oversight of industries favored by President Donald Trump. Stocks rallied Wednesday as traders bet gridlock would limit major policy changes and falling oil prices would ease inflation. Bitwise Europe analyst André Dragosch also told Decrypt the Democratic sweep was "largely priced in."

Thursday brought a different interpretation. Investors weighed what Democratic control might mean for the data-center boom, particularly in states where voters oppose rising electricity costs and new facilities.

And the new political configuration is already moving things. Voters in at least 29 U.S. jurisdictions backed restrictions on data-center development, although not every measure is binding. UBS estimates potential pauses could threaten 30% of planned U.S. data-center capacity additions in 2028, per Reuters.

"We suspect there is going to be some scrutiny on this aggressive AI buildout," Swissquote senior analyst Ipek Ozkardeskaya told Reuters.

Chipmakers and AI infrastructure companies took the hit first. The Philadelphia Semiconductor Index dropped about 8%, while CoreWeave and Nebius fell by double digits. Tesla also tumbled roughly 9.5%, extending losses after Elon Musk spent heavily backing Republican candidates. Investors were also questioning the returns on massive AI investments.

The S&P 500 had the biggest bearish intraday movement in months, dropping almost 4% in minutes.

"The market's moving from the promise of AI to the price of AI," Ted Parkhill, chief executive of Incline Investment Management, told Reuters.

The political shift also threatens crypto's ambitions in Washington. Democratic control complicates efforts to revive the stalled Clarity Act, a proposed federal rulebook for digital assets. The new Congress will not take office until January. Politics alone cannot explain Bitcoin losing thousands of dollars in an hour.

Wednesday's rally looked dramatic, but much of its buying pressure came from traders closing losing bets against Bitcoin. About $378 million in short positions were liquidated in one hour as Bitcoin pushed above $80,000, according to CoinGlass data.

Today’s reversal flipped the liquidation machinery in the opposite direction. Exchanges automatically close underfunded longs as prices fall, generating more selling and the enormous red candles visible on Bitcoin's hourly chart.

Crypto was already vulnerable before the election. The market had lost roughly $300 billion in value over the previous month, amid concerns about AI spending, rising borrowing costs and the fallout from last week's multibillion-dollar Binance hack.

Traders also expect another Federal Reserve rate increase in December as inflation persists. Friday's October jobs report could alter those bets; higher rates generally hurt demand for speculative investments.

Technically, Bitcoin's still-forming daily candle is threatening a bearish engulfing pattern, potentially swallowing Wednesday's green body and erasing the breakout in a single session.

The hourly chart shows selling through $79,000 to near $76,100. The next test is $75,000, while reclaiming $80,000 could ease the pressure—but Thursday's candle has not closed, so the pattern remains unconfirmed.

#5110. Bull Bitcoin Becomes First Exchange to Add Payjoin Privacy Deposits, Withdrawals, and Payjoin-by-Default Trading

Bull Bitcoin announced deep Payjoin integration with its exchange, claiming to be the first Bitcoin exchange in the world to offer Payjoin as a deposit and withdrawal option. The update unlocks two new concepts: “Payjoin-by-default and Payjoin trading,” according to he press release shared with Bitcoin Magazine.

Founded by Francis Pouliot in Canada in 2013, Bull Bitcoin is the world’s longest-running Bitcoin-only exchange. Its non-custodial platform lets customers in Canada, Europe, and Latin America buy Bitcoin directly into their own wallet and sell Bitcoin to their bank account. Bull Bitcoin also develops BULL Wallet, a free and open-source self-custodial Bitcoin wallet. The company has been self-funded since its inception.

Every time a BULL Wallet user buys or sells Bitcoin on-chain via their built-in exchange, that transaction will be a Payjoin by default. This will allow Bitcoin buyers to passively aggregate their wallet’s UTXOs, which can reduce their Bitcoin mining fees in the future. “Many Bitcoin users do not even know that they must periodically “merge” their coins by spending them to a new address in their Bitcoin wallet, if they want to reduce the economic costs of future Bitcoin transactions,” explained the press release. Payjoin-by-default automates this process. Not only does this help reduce the size of Bitcoin’s UTXO set, making it easier for everyone to run a Bitcoin node, but it also reduces fee pressure for every Bitcoin user.

Payjoin trading is a novel concept that protects users from malicious actors trying to gain insights into their financial habits by conducting chain analysis. Because transaction amounts in Bitcoin are public, it’s possible to reverse-engineer information such as someone’s salary or trading strategies such as dollar-cost averaging. Bull Bitcoin is a non-custodial exchange, so every trade leaves an on-chain trail.

In December 2024, BULL Wallet became the world’s first Bitcoin wallet to integrate the Payjoin V2 protocol. This was a highly experimental project coordinated with Dan Gould and the Payjoin Dev Kit team, who have since founded the Payjoin Foundation.

Researchers have used the insights from this experimentation to improve the Payjoin experience to make it more seamless and resistant to attacks. The software powering Payjoin, both for the wallet and for the exchange, is fully free and open-source.

To benefit from these features, all a user has to do is download BULL Wallet, create a Bull Bitcoin exchange account, and log into their account via the BULL mobile app; “The rest happens automatically, like magic!”

#5109. Anchorage Digital seeks CFTC swap dealer registration for crypto options business

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

The firm plans to offer European-style bitcoin and ether options with maturities of up to one year if its application is approved.

Institutional crypto platform Anchorage Digital has filed an application to register a swap dealer with the Commodity Futures Trading Commission as it looks to expand its institutional crypto options business.

The application was submitted to the National Futures Association through Anchorage Digital Swap Dealer, LLC, according to a statement shared exclusively with The Block.

Anchorage is now awaiting registration while the NFA reviews its application. CEO and co-founder Nathan McCauley offered no estimate of when that process might finish, but said the company would share further updates as appropriate. "We aren't in a position to speculate on timing," he told The Block.

If the application is approved, McCauley said Anchorage Digital Swap Dealer expects to offer European-style options on bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) and ether Ethereum (ETH), with maturities ranging up to one year. The firm intends to add more products later, he added, subject to its own approval process and regulatory requirements.

McCauley said that once registration is complete, institutional clients would be able to trade digital asset derivatives with a CFTC-registered counterparty alongside use of Anchorage Digital's existing custody and other services.

Much of Anchorage's existing options business growth has come from public companies holding bitcoin and ether on their balance sheets, the firm said in the statement, while asset managers and funds using options to manage their exposure have also contributed. McCauley would not disclose specific trading volumes or growth figures when asked by The Block, and declined to explain how Anchorage had previously arranged options trades for clients.

"What we can say is that institutional demand for regulated access to digital asset derivatives continues to grow, and establishing a dedicated swap dealer entity is part of how we're building the infrastructure to meet it," he said.

Anchorage Digital Bank, N.A. became the first federally chartered digital asset bank in 2021 and is regulated by the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency. The swap dealer entity would face CFTC requirements for capital, risk management, and margin, using the same framework the Dodd-Frank Act applied to the banks for interest rate, credit, and FX markets, the firm said.

"It will bring our options business under the same federal framework as the dealers they already face," McCauley said in the statement.

In March, Anchorage Digital added collateral management to its Atlas platform, including 24/7 monitoring and automated margin calls for loans, derivatives, and other institutional transactions, The Block reported at the time. It also acquired Securitize's wealth management unit in December to expand its federally regulated crypto services for registered investment advisors.

#5104. Greece Approves 10% Crypto Capital Gains Tax as First Framework Lands Below Draft Rate

Greece's parliament approved a 10% capital gains tax on cryptocurrencies, the country's first dedicated crypto tax framework, with annual gains up to €500 exempt.

Greece’s parliament approved on Thursday a bill that imposes a 10% capital gains tax on cryptocurrencies, the first comprehensive legal framework for taxing crypto assets in the country.

Cryptocurrency capital gains of up to €500 ($563) a year will be exempt from the new tax, the bill says. For bitcoin holders in Greece, that threshold is a small buffer, not a carve-out for larger positions.

“Further implementation directives will be published in the coming weeks, which will also define transparency and audit rules,” a senior finance ministry official told Reuters.

An earlier draft circulated in October had floated a 15% rate. The version lawmakers passed lands five points lower, while keeping the €500 annual exemption that was already in that proposal.

The tax is set to apply from Jan. 1, 2027, Greek coverage of the finance ministry bill says. It is levied on the net gain — the difference between the acquisition price and the transfer price — not on the full sale proceeds. Trading fees and other costs tied to the deal can factor into that calculation.

A taxable transfer is not limited to selling for euros. Using cryptocurrency to buy goods or services, or contributing it to cover or increase company capital, can also crystallize a gain under the new rules. That matters for anyone in Greece who pays a merchant in bitcoin rather than converting first.

Simply holding bitcoin while its euro value rises does not, by itself, create a tax bill. Crypto-to-crypto swaps, such as bitcoin for another token, are treated as non-taxable events in the explanatory material that accompanied the bill, matching the approach France has used to avoid taxing every hop between coins.

Net annual gains at or below €500 are exempt. Greek analyses of the text say that once net gains clear that ceiling, the 10% rate applies to the full net gain for the year, not only the slice above €500. Losses above €500 in a tax year may be carried forward for up to five years and offset only against future crypto capital gains.

Purchases of crypto assets are also being pulled into Greece’s existing “source of funds” checks from 2027, so buyers may need to show that the euros used to acquire bitcoin or other tokens came from declared income or other lawful capital. The rule does not add a separate purchase tax; it widens the audit trail around how coins were funded.

European Union countries do not share a unified tax system for the sector. Rates on cryptocurrency gains typically run from about 8% to 30% across member states and are usually levied on capital gains, the finance ministry official said. Greece’s 10% sits toward the softer end of that band, below France’s often-cited 30% treatment and above Cyprus’s 8%.

The same patchwork is shifting elsewhere in the bloc. The Netherlands has moved toward a realized capital gains tax from 2028, while Germany and Portugal still exempt crypto held longer than a year. Since January, the EU’s DAC8 reporting rules have required crypto platforms to send user and transaction data to national tax authorities — a tool Greece already wrote into domestic law.

State revenue from the new levy could not be projected, the official said, because the vast majority of investors use platforms abroad, leaving the size of Greece’s cryptocurrency market hard to measure.

For bitcoin holders who trade on foreign exchanges, that gap is the practical test. The rate is now set in law; whether Athens can match 10% on paper with enforceable cost-basis data from offshore platforms is the question the coming directives will have to answer.

#5103. Democrats to Push Broader Crypto Market Structure Bill After Sweeping US Midterms: Report

Democrats to Push Broader Crypto Market Structure Bill After Sweeping US Midterms: Report

House Democrats are reportedly set to progress crypto legislation after sweeping the U.S. midterms.

There are plans to expand the Crypto Investor Consumer Bill of Rights—a Clarity Act amendment led by Dem. Maxine Waters—into a broader market structure bill, journalist Eleanor Terrett tweeted. The proposal would include giving the SEC more power to supervise the crypto markets and limiting the role of the CFTC, sources told Terrett.

🚨SCOOP: House Democrats are discussing plans to expand the Crypto Investor and Consumer Bill of Rights, @RepMaxineWaters’ amendment to the Clarity Act in 2025, into a broader crypto “market structure” bill next Congress.

The proposal would fold in Democratic Clarity Act…

— Eleanor Terrett (@EleanorTerrett) November 4, 2026

On the surface, the midterm election results could be seen as a major hit to crypto policy in the U.S., with President Trump's Republican administration widely regarded as the pro-crypto party, while the Democrats were deemed more hostile. However, experts, told Decrypt, the tide might be turning and crypto policy evolving into a more bipartisan issue.

Advocacy organization Stand With Crypto endorsed 45 candidates in the midterms, per its election tracker, with 27 pro-crypto candidate wins, 12 losses, and six races still live at the time of writing. Nearly half of the endorsed candidates were Democrats.

"We’re proud to see a majority of our endorsed candidates win their elections," Mason Lynaugh, executive director of Stand With Crypto, said in a statement shared with Decrypt. "To the candidates who won’t return to Washington, thank you for going to bat for crypto-owning Americans when it counted."

Congratulations to the pro-crypto champions who won their elections, including more than 50 pro-crypto new members heading to Congress.

In the closest races, our advocates showed up: in Florida's 9th District, Darren Soto won by 1,477 votes. In that same district, we mobilized… pic.twitter.com/lcq055GNET

— Stand With Crypto🛡️ (@standwithcrypto) November 4, 2026

James Butterfill, head of research at CoinShares, described Republican Bryan Steil as a "meaningful loss" for crypto. The Wisconsin Congressman was the chair of the House digital assets subcommittee and closely involved in efforts to advance the Clarity Act—which were stymied when it stalled in the Senate earlier this year.

That said, Butterfill also highlighted several winners—Democrats Chris Pappas in New Hampshire and James Talarico in Texas, as well as Wyoming Republican Harriet Hageman—who have supported crypto policy. He stressed, however, that the challenge is now "converting" that support into passing legislation. Some believe that could slow an already difficult path for the Clarity Act.

"I wouldn’t say it’s game over for Clarity, but passing it has become tougher," Carlos Guzman, vice president of research at crypto trading firm GSR, told Decrypt. "Some Democrats support clearer crypto rules, but I wouldn’t expect it to be high on their agenda. Any agreement will likely require stronger provisions on things like consumer protection and conflicts of interest."

The Clarity Act looks to establish rules for crypto markets and has been batted back and forth since 2025. Democrats have pushed back on the bill, citing Trump's crypto conflicts of interest, with Senator Elizabeth Warren (D-MA) pushing for the bill to ban officials and their families from profiting from the sector.

"For other crypto policies, the election doesn’t automatically replace the leadership of the SEC or CFTC or stop their rulemaking," Guzman said. "Congressional oversight does change, however, so we might see more scrutiny of regulatory decisions, with Democrats also able to influence agency funding and future appointments."

Butterfill added that he believes crypto is becoming less dependent on the Clarity Act, as the SEC and CFTC push for regulatory clarity without the need for Congress. He also said that AI regulation may take eyes away from crypto, slowing crypto policy in the process.

Following the midterms, Bitcoin has exhibited volatility, and is currently trading at around $76,820, down 4.1% on the day, per Decrypt data.

"The result alone would not make me change my medium-term Bitcoin outlook," Butterfill told Decrypt. "Slower reform could delay some institutional activity, but we would expect the more direct impact to fall on US crypto businesses reliant on new regulatory permissions. We would revisit the outlook if political uncertainty materially weakened investment flows or tightened financial conditions."

#5101. Europe’s largest asset manager Amundi tokenizes equity index fund on Ethereum

The new share class offers blockchain-based exposure to an index tracking 600 companies across European markets.

Amundi, Europe’s largest fund manager by assets, has launched a tokenized share class of its STOXX Europe 600 equity index fund on Ethereum, expanding its blockchain-based investment offerings beyond money market funds.

The new share class was issued on Ethereum in collaboration with CACEIS, a European asset-servicing company that provided the tokenization infrastructure. The fund will continue to be available through traditional investment channels, Amundi said in a Thursday announcement.

The fund tracks the STOXX Europe 600 Index, which covers 600 large-, mid- and small-cap companies across developed European markets. Amundi said it plans to develop a broader range of tokenized funds for institutional clients and digital platforms, citing potential improvements in accessibility, settlement speed and operational efficiency.

The launch follows the asset manager’s introduction of its first tokenized money market fund share class in November 2025, also developed with CACEIS on Ethereum.

Related: Robinhood’s Tenev warns US tokenization uncertainty deters ‘serious assets’

Amundi’s move comes amid rapid growth in the global tokenized equities market, although the largest products remain concentrated in US-related assets.

The value of tokenized stocks reached $4.24 billion as of Wednesday, Nov. 4, up nearly 27% over the past 30 days, according to RWA.xyz data. The platform’s 10 largest tokenized equity products by value were all tied to US companies or US-listed securities, including Securitize, SpaceX, Circle and Alphabet.

Meanwhile, European financial firms have pressed regulators to accelerate reforms. In April, a coalition of 39 companies and industry groups warned that the EU risked falling behind the US in tokenized finance, calling for expanded asset eligibility, higher volume limits and the removal of time restrictions on licenses under the bloc’s Distributed Ledger Technology (DLT) Pilot Regime.

#5100. Base deploys Denim upgrade on mainnet, replacing Flashblocks with 200ms block times

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Denim retires Flashblocks, adds millisecond-resolution timestamps, and updates B20 transfer and compliance controls.

Base's fourth mainnet upgrade, Denim, is live, with the Coinbase-incubated Layer 2 now producing a full canonical block every 200 milliseconds, or five per second, the project said in a statement shared with The Block on Thursday.

The upgrade also introduces millisecond-resolution timestamps for smart contracts, alongside new transfer protections and compliance controls for tokens using Base's B20 standard.

Base said the upgrade retires Flashblocks and changes how the pending block tag works, making block production "simpler, more consistent, and more reliable." Each block now follows its parent exactly 200 milliseconds later, with the protocol enforcing the interval rather than relying on the sequencer's clock.

Applications that previously relied on Flashblocks must migrate to standard canonical block subscriptions, according to the statement.

"Base’s north star is to bring the world onchain, starting with trading, financing, and payments," the statement read. "Those use cases need a chain that updates state often and lets builders reason about time precisely."

According to the statement, the upgrade also introduces BaseTime, which gives smart contracts access to Unix timestamps in milliseconds. Ethereum-compatible block.timestamp remains unchanged at whole-second resolution, while new RPC fields provide millisecond timestamps for blocks, transactions, and logs.

Denim also introduces reusable compliance policies through a NOT operator and extends transfer-executor restrictions to direct transfers. It also blocks transfers, mints and seizures that send tokens to the token contract's own address, Base said.

The upgrade follows Azul, Beryl and Cobalt, Base's first three independent upgrades since its 2023 launch. Azul introduced a new client stack and multiproof system, while Beryl brought the B20 token standard. Cobalt added conditional transactions and expanded B20 compliance features.

#5097. Core Scientific (CORZ) to Exit Bitcoin Mining by Year-End as Colocation Hits $215 Million

Core Scientific will complete its exit from bitcoin mining by year-end and enter 2027 as a pure-play high-density colocation provider, management said on Thursday’s third-quarter earnings call, capping a pivot that has already cut the company’s mining fleet nearly in half.

Chief Financial Officer Jim Nygaard told investors the company expects to “complete the wind-down during the fourth quarter.” Chief Executive Officer Adam Sullivan put the same end date more bluntly: “We expect to complete our exit from Bitcoin mining by year end.” The comments landed alongside a results release filed with the SEC that shows how far the shift has already gone.

Colocation revenue climbed nearly 60% sequentially to $215.4 million in the quarter ended Sept. 30, the earnings exhibit shows. Digital asset self-mining revenue fell to $12.3 million, down about 79% from $57.4 million a year earlier. Hosted mining for third parties added just $5.2 million. Of $232.9 million in total revenue, colocation now accounts for roughly 92%.

Self-mining posted a $7.5 million gross loss in the quarter. Colocation gross profit was $128.4 million, a 60% margin. Adjusted EBITDA more than tripled sequentially to $133.6 million, with the margin expanding to 57% from 25%.

Nygaard said Core Scientific had nearly 50% fewer miners online at the end of September than at year-end 2025. The company has been repurposing power and floor space under long-term customer agreements for AI and high-performance computing workloads, a process its investor materials describe as continuing “as circumstances allow.”

That path is not unique among public miners. Peers have been selling bitcoin and leasing campuses to AI tenants, and research notes have flagged a widening funding gap between those that can finance data center builds and those that cannot.

Core Scientific is billing for 485 megawatts of colocation capacity as of Sept. 30 — up 90 MW in the quarter — representing more than $700 million in average annualized colocation GAAP revenue. The remaining roughly 100 MW under its CoreWeave buildout is in commissioning and expected to start recognizing revenue over the next few months, Sullivan said.

Beyond CoreWeave’s contracted 590 MW, Core Scientific has 530 MW under an AMD-backed agreement across five campuses, with initial delivery targeted for the first half of 2027 and full delivery by the end of 2028. Together, the two relationships cover about 1.1 GW. Once fully delivered and billing, management expects that portfolio alone to generate $1.8 billion in average annual run-rate revenue and $1.6 billion in net operating income.

Bitcoin is almost gone from the books. Digital assets stood at $2.7 million on Sept. 30, down from $49.7 million three months earlier and $222 million at year-end 2025. Earlier this year, Core Scientific was still adding coins even as it sold into the pivot; that chapter is effectively closed.

The mining exit has already carried a cash cost. A $41.9 million loss on contract termination booked in the second quarter reflects the decision to end a Block/Proto mining chip supply agreement. Liquidity ended the third quarter at about $1.7 billion, including roughly $800 million in cash, after heavy capital spending on AMD sites and the Polaris acquisition.

Sullivan framed the wind-down as simplifying the business toward “more attractive long-term growth opportunities.” Nygaard added that starting 2027 with a “very clean P&L” — one without mining in the numbers — should give investors a clearer look at a true colocation run-rate.

The test now is whether Core Scientific turns the last machines off on schedule in the fourth quarter, and whether the AMD financing package lands in time to keep that clean P&L growing.

The declines reversed Wednesday's rally, when bitcoin climbed back above $80,000 following the U.S. midterm elections and signs that the Binance hack had not caused broader market disruption.

Midterm election results appeared to generate short-term optimism, but analysts say they haven't resolved macroeconomic uncertainties.

As of 2 p.m. ET, the Dow Jones Industrial Average and S&P 500 were down about 3% for the day, while the tech-heavy Nasdaq Composite had dropped 4.7%. The Cboe Volatility Index, or VIX, surged approximately 60%, reflecting heightened investor anxiety.

The selloff also triggered a wave of forced liquidations across crypto derivatives markets. About $879.92 million in positions were liquidated over a four-hour period, including $818.90 million in longs and $61.02 million in shorts, according to CoinGlass. Total liquidations over the past 24 hours reached nearly $1.1 billion, affecting nearly 211,000 traders.

It should be noted that liquidation figures are typically based on publicly available data, which may significantly understate the extent of market liquidations.

The reversal suggests Wednesday's rebound may have been driven more by temporary optimism than a meaningful improvement in the market's underlying conditions, according to Wintermute.

The trading firm said the rally reflected a combination of relief following the Binance hack, enthusiasm surrounding the election results, optimism about a potential slowdown in AI spending and falling oil prices. However, those factors proved short-lived as broader macroeconomic pressures persisted.

"It's probably a combination of all of them in some way. What matters is that all of them seemed to be so short-lived that it actually didn't really matter," said Jasper De Maere, an OTC trader at Wintermute. "Trump is still the president for two years, the Middle East is still escalating, inflation is elevated into a strong U.S. economy."

Beyond the immediate market pressures, the election results may also complicate the outlook for crypto regulation, potentially tempering some of the optimism that helped drive Wednesday's rebound.

Paul Howard, senior director at Wincent, said the Democrats' strengthened position in Congress could make bipartisan cooperation necessary for meaningful progress on crypto legislation over the next two years. With the Clarity Act's prospects uncertain, Howard expects the existing regulatory framework to largely remain intact until the next election cycle.

Ryan Kirkley, CEO of Global Settlement Network, said the election could delay crypto legislation but wouldn't fundamentally change longer-term institutional demand for digital assets.

"Investors are still focused on liquidity, interest rates and the broader economy, and those factors will continue to influence the market regardless of which party controls Congress," Kirkley said.

If the weakness continues, Wintermute identified $73,000 and $68,000 as the next levels to watch for bitcoin. The firm said it would need to see bitcoin convincingly reclaim and hold $80,000 before turning more positive.

#5094. Solana Labs’ Leap Launches Mobile App for Trading Tokenized Stocks and Crypto

Solana Labs’ Leap Launches Mobile App for Trading Tokenized Stocks and Crypto

Solana Labs is betting the network’s next wave of traders wants more than meme coins.

Leap, a new company under Solana Labs, launched a beta of its mobile trading app Thursday, letting users trade tokenized stocks, ETFs, metals, currencies, and crypto in one place, around the clock and subject to restrictions.

The app is available in early access on iPhone through Apple’s public TestFlight program, with an Android version coming soon. It isn’t available in the U.K. or other restricted regions.

Leap is built by the team behind Dialect, which Solana Labs acquired earlier this year. Dialect developed Solana Actions and Blinks, a protocol that turns blockchain transactions into shareable links, which launched in 2024, as well as the cashtags feature on X.

The company is pitching the app as an alternative to fast-paced meme coin trading, aimed at people who want to make better-informed decisions over the long term. Its research team curates market-moving news and pins each story to the price chart at the moment it broke.

The app tracks profit and loss across every deposit, withdrawal, trade and fee, including each position’s breakeven price. Users enter an amount in dollars, and each trade executes as an onchain swap.

Leap uses Jupiter for trade routing, Helius for infrastructure and Birdeye for market data.

“The Solana blockchain now confirms a trade in about 150 milliseconds thanks to Alpenglow, so the network is now faster than ever,” said Chris Osborn, Leap’s general manager and Dialect’s founder. Alpenglow a consensus overhaul designed to sharply cut Solana’s confirmation times.

“Every app that brings new people onchain makes the whole ecosystem stronger,” said Solana Labs co-founder and CEO Anatoly Yakovenko.

Leap isn’t charging its own fees at launch, though network and routing costs apply. It plans to use future fees to buy and burn SOL, permanently removing tokens from circulation, and to fund a referral program. Early waitlist members, dubbed the Founders Club, would earn 30% of the trading fees generated by users they invite and 5% from those users’ own referrals.

Leap arrives as tokenized stocks gain traction on Solana and beyond. Securitize has brought tokenized Nvidia, Apple, and Amazon shares to Solana. The trend has also met pushback: 56 companies have objected to having their shares traded on OKX and ICE’s planned tokenized stock venue.

#5092. Hyperliquid Strategies files to become CFTC-registered FCM, posts $13.3 million in quaterly revenue

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

The filing would let the HYPE treasury firm carry U.S. client accounts for HIP-3 perpetual futures markets, pending regulatory approval.

Nasdaq-listed Hyperliquid Strategies (PURR) reported $13.3 million in revenue for the quarter ended Sept. 30, exceeding analyst estimates of $10.6 million to $12 million, depending on the data provider.

The company posted $620.6 million in net income, largely driven by $803 million in unrealized gains on its Hyperliquid (HYPE) token holdings, partially offset by a $172.3 million income tax provision.

Perhaps most notably, Hyperliquid Strategies filed with the CFTC to become a futures commission merchant (FCM), which would allow the firm to carry U.S. client accounts for HIP-3 markets.

"Hyperliquid recently announced its intention to add additional deployer functionalities under the name HIP-3," the firm said in a release. "These optional additional features would allow deployers to build and launch permissioned markets on Hyperliquid's onchain infrastructure. Independent operators would run their own market in a compliant manner, while the chain underneath would stay a neutral infrastructure layer."

The FCM, which is subject to regulatory approval, would allow Hyperliquid Strategies to "play a role" in bringing perpetual futures to U.S. customers within a regulated framework.

"There can be no assurance as to the timing or outcome of the application or regulatory approval of permissioned HIP-3 markets," the firm said.

This comes as Hyperliquid's underlying infrastructure moves closer to the U.S. market. NYSE parent Intercontinental Exchange agreed last week to partner with TradeXYZ to bring Hyperliquid-based perp futures markets to U.S. traders. ICE would operate the exchange and clearing infrastructure, with TradeXYZ designing the markets.

Earlier this year, President Donald Trump said his administration was working to bring Hyperliquid into the U.S. "in a fully compliant and legal fashion."

#5091. Bitcoin Price Trades Near $76,000 as Nasdaq Tumbles into Correction

Bitcoin price traded near $76,000 on Thursday afternoon, down more than 5% on the day after sliding through the mid-$75,000s as a Nasdaq-led selloff in chip and AI stocks deepened into a formal correction and forced out more than $1 billion in leveraged bets, Bitcoin Magazine Pro data shows.

The afternoon session extended the morning crash that had already erased overnight gains above $80,000. Bitcoin briefly tested support near $75,400 before stabilizing just under $76,000 into the final hour of U.S. cash trading.

Bitcoin near $75,900 in afternoon trade, on track for its biggest single-day slide since June. The move undid Wednesday’s short-squeeze bounce in a single session. Traders who bought the midterm rebound with borrowed money were the first forced out when stocks cracked at the open, and the bitcoin price never regained the $77,000 area that held as morning support.

Wall Street set the tone. The Nasdaq Composite fell about 5% on Thursday and dropped into correction territory, more than 10% below the record close it set last month, CNBC reported. The S&P 500 lost roughly 4%, and the Dow shed more than 1,600 points.

Semiconductors led the rout. The Philadelphia Semiconductor Index sank about 11%, and the iShares Semiconductor ETF posted its biggest one-day drop since March 2020. Nvidia and Broadcom each fell around 9%, Qualcomm lost about 9% and Tesla dropped about 11%.

Investors are pricing in a slower AI data-center buildout under a Democratic Congress. Traders fear construction pauses and tighter financing for neoclouds, a risk that has weighed on chip and infrastructure names since the midterm results. Analysts told CNBC that the $1.2 trillion of AI capital spending forecast for next year is now being reworked.

Bitcoin has moved with that AI-heavy complex for most of the year. Miners that sold bitcoin to fund data centers are now caught in both trades, so political risk on power and construction hits the same names that used to trade mainly on the bitcoin price.

Forced selling kept the bitcoin price under pressure into the afternoon. Roughly $1.08 billion in leveraged crypto positions were liquidated over 24 hours, including about $954 million in long bets, as futures markets unwound positions built during Wednesday’s short squeeze.

Nearly $900 million of that wipeout came in a roughly four-hour stretch after the U.S. open, turning a stock-led break into a cascade of margin calls. Ether fell below $2,300 as the same leverage flushed out.

Crypto equities followed. Strategy (MSTR), Coinbase (COIN) and Circle (CRCL) each fell more than 10%, and bitcoin miners dropped as much as 20%. Keel Infrastructure (KEEL), which sold its entire bitcoin treasury for $192 million, slid about 20%. CleanSpark (CLSK), Cipher (CIFR) and TeraWulf (WULF) each lost more than 12%.

The next test is Friday’s October jobs report, then October CPI on Nov. 10 and the Fed’s Dec. 9 decision. Policy risk is building too, with House Democrats drafting their own market structure bill ahead of taking the committees in January.

Traders are watching whether the bitcoin price can hold the mid-$75,000 area that served as support on election night and several times since August. A clean break would leave bitcoin with room to run lower, near its weakest levels in months and put the September low back in view.

Options markets have been quiet into the jobs print, but the cash market is not. Volume stayed elevated through the afternoon as stocks and crypto sold together rather than against each other.

At the time of writing, the bitcoin price is near $75,900.

#5089. Fed, OCC check US banks after Binance hack as experts flag security risks

US regulators reportedly contacted banks following last week’s $6.3 billion Binance security hack, while experts raised concerns about transaction security and crypto risks.

US regulators reportedly contacted banks following last week’s $6.3 billion Binance hack, as cybersecurity experts raised concerns about how financial institutions handle crypto-related security risks.

Officials from the Federal Reserve and Office of the Comptroller of the Currency (OCC) began contacting banks on Monday to ask about “unusual activity” or service disruptions, Reuters reported Thursday, citing people familiar with the matter.

The reported inquiries could be followed by closer attention to how banks manage crypto-related risks, including custody, counterparty exposure and incident response, according to Stefan Muehlbauer, head of US government affairs at CertiK.

“After an incident of this scale, it would be surprising if the Fed and OCC didn’t check in with the banks they supervise,” Muehlbauer told Cointelegraph. “Their mandate is to protect the financial system, and that means understanding where exposures sit and how quickly they can respond.”

According to Reuters, banks have been assessing the hack and checking their systems to figure out whether they could absorb or manage similar threats.

Reuters’ report also noted that a cybersecurity team at one of the largest US banks contacted Binance within hours of the hack. A Binance spokesperson confirmed with Cointelegraph that the exchange shared its publicly released incident report with all its banking partners and was not aware of any vulnerabilities affecting them.

Muehlbauer explained the procedure currently unfolding is “how a maturing framework should work.” He added that “it’s not a signal that regulators are pulling back from the industry.”

The inquiries also come after the Federal Reserve removed a requirement in April 2025 for state member banks to notify regulators before engaging in crypto activities. The Fed said it would continue overseeing those activities through its regular supervisory process.

A Federal Reserve spokesperson told Cointelegraph that the agency had nothing to share regarding the reported inquiries. Cointelegraph also contacted the OCC for comment but has not heard back at the time of publishing.

Related: Binance traces $6.3B hack to fake job interviews, compromised AI tool

Binance said in its Nov. 3 incident report that approximately $6.34 billion in digital assets were affected by the attack on Oct. 29, with $2.85 billion frozen or intercepted as of the report.

According to the crypto exchange, the attack began with compromised software installed by an engineer in June. Hackers later employed social engineering, stolen credentials and malicious software to get access to wallet systems and carry out unauthorized transfers.

Dr. Darren Williams, founder and CEO of cybersecurity firm BlackFog, told Cointelegraph that the methods used in the attack pose potential risks to traditional banks, particularly those offering crypto custody and payment services.

“Attackers no longer need to break encryption or steal private keys if they can compromise the trusted systems and people authorized to move money,” Williams said.

He warned that hackers could use compromised employee devices, accounts or third-party services to move money without triggering any alerts. He also said the growing use of agentic AI in financial operations could pose even more risks.

“The priority must be stopping unauthorized transactions and data exfiltration before assets or sensitive information leave the organization, not simply investigating the damage afterwards,” Williams said.

Binance has since said that it has strengthened its security controls and was bringing in independent cybersecurity experts to review the attack.

#5087. Hollywood Studios Quietly Ban Staff From Betting on Prediction Markets: Report

Hollywood Studios Quietly Ban Staff From Betting on Prediction Markets: Report

Hollywood studios and production companies have been quietly issuing guidelines barring employees from betting on prediction markets, entertainment industry trade paper The Hollywood Reporter reported on Thursday.

The warnings prohibit staff from betting on events such as studio earnings, casting decisions and release dates, or passing information to friends and family so they can bet instead, according to the report. At least one company has reportedly set up a hotline for employees to anonymously report colleagues suspected of placing bets.

The Hollywood Reporter's own parent company, Penske Media, has also updated its employee handbook in recent weeks to ban bets on "award nominations, casting decisions, production timelines and release dates," the outlet said.

The rules extend long-standing bans on insider trading in the stock market to platforms such as Kalshi and Polymarket, which let users bet on everything from who will be cast as the next James Bond to who will be voted off a reality show.

"You now have, essentially, an explosion in the number of employers who have to think about insider trading rules from an ethical standpoint," Joshua Mitts, a law professor at Columbia University who studies prediction markets, told the outlet.

Enforcing those rules may be another matter. "With blockchain-based trading, it can be basically impossible if you know how to use a mixer correctly and know how to hide your identity behind multiple layers of intermediation," Mitts said.

Some bookmakers are already spooked. Betting site BetOnline temporarily pulled its market on the next James Bond this week after a sudden wave of "sharp money" pushed actor Callum Turner above longtime favorite Jack Lowden, raising concerns about a leak, according to the report.

Entertainment insiders have been caught before. In February, Kalshi said a video editor at MrBeast's company had made about $5,000 using confidential information to bet on MrBeast content; he was fired and fined $20,000.

Federal prosecutors have also brought cases over insider trading on prediction markets. In April, an active-duty U.S. soldier was charged with using classified information about the raid to capture Nicolás Maduro to bet on Polymarket. In May, a Google engineer was charged with commodities fraud, wire fraud and money laundering over Polymarket bets allegedly placed using confidential company data. Both cases are pending.

Governments have moved too. California, New York and Illinois have banned state employees from using nonpublic information to bet on prediction markets, while the platforms have rolled out their own surveillance tools. Polymarket has investigated dozens of bets on the U.S. midterm elections.

One kind of entertainment bet is already off the table. Under the Onion Futures Act of 1958, U.S. law explicitly bans just two types of futures contracts: those on onions and those on movie box office receipts, The Hollywood Reporter noted.

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

IREN has been shifting its focus to the AI infrastructure business, and its AI cloud revenue rose 182% in the past quarter to $200 million.

IREN (IREN) reported bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) mining revenue of $28 million in the three months ended Sept. 30, which is a 58% quarter-over-quarter decline from $67 million and an 88% drop from $233 million a year earlier.

According to the company's 10-Q filing, IREN mined 402 BTC during the period, compared with 2,039 BTC in the same quarter last year. Average operating hashrate fell to 8.8 EH/s from 45.3 EH/s year-over-year. IREN held no bitcoin on its balance sheet as of the end of September as it liquidates mined bitcoin daily.

"We continue to operate Bitcoin miners at certain of our data centers while we transition this data center capacity toward AI Cloud," IREN wrote in the filing. "We aim to substantially complete this transition by December 31, 2026, with immaterial Bitcoin mining operations expected to continue through calendar year 2027."

IREN, which launched in 2018, announced in March last year that it will stop expanding its bitcoin mining operations and shift its focus to AI and high-performance computing through AI cloud services and data center businesses. Competing bitcoin miner firms MARA and CleanSpark have also turned to the AI infrastructure business.

Consequently, the company's quarterly revenue rose 66% sequentially to $228 million, driven by AI cloud revenue of around $200 million, which is up 182% from the prior quarter. The company posted a net loss of $263 million, narrower than the $684 million loss in the previous quarter, while adjusted EBITDA rose to $66 million from $19 million.

IREN said it has $20.6 billion in total assets, including $6 billion of data center assets, with assets and revenue underpinned by customer contracts with Nvidia, Microsoft, and 13 other customers.

The firm's Nasdaq-listed shares fell 17% on Thursday to close at $18.03, in line with a broader sell-off across AI and tech stocks.

#5080. S&P 500 Plunges, Wall Street Fears Circuit Breaker, Just One Day After Trump Celebrated Record Highs

S&P 500 Plunges, Wall Street Fears Circuit Breaker, Just One Day After Trump Celebrated Record Highs

President Donald Trump's celebration of Wall Street's highs aged rather poorly. Less than 24 hours after boasting about Americans' retirement accounts going "through the roof," the S&P 500 plunged as much as 3.8% Thursday, bringing emergency trading halts into the conversation.

The selloff prompted traders to monitor the market's circuit-breaker thresholds, which can temporarily shut down trading across U.S. stock exchanges when losses become too severe. The S&P 500 would need to fall another 3.2 percentage points from its decline to trigger the first emergency halt.

The timing is particularly awkward for Trump, who has repeatedly pointed to stock market performance as evidence of his administration's economic success.

"Stock Market just hit another Record High—91 Days in just a short period of time—Less than two years. Your 401(k)s are through the roof," Trump wrote Wednesday on his Truth Social account. He closed the message with "Record Setting All! President DONALD J. TRUMP."

Wall Street had rallied Wednesday following the Democratic Party's sweeping midterm election victories. The S&P 500 gained 1.6%, its strongest daily performance in three months, while the Dow Jones Industrial Average jumped more than 1,500 points to 53.361 points.

Thursday's losses erase that rally in a single session, with technology stocks taking the biggest beating.

The U.S. stock market has three emergency thresholds designed to slow panic selling. These mechanisms, known as circuit breakers, automatically suspend trading when the S&P 500 falls by a specified percentage from its previous closing price.

The first threshold is a 7% decline, corresponding to an S&P 500 level of 7,150.41 points for Thursday's session. Reaching that level before 3:25 p.m. ET would trigger a 15-minute halt across US equity markets.

The circuit breaker level is located in the following chart’s blue line

A 13% decline, or 6,689.09 points, would trigger another 15-minute suspension. The most severe threshold comes at 20%, or 6,150.89 points, which would shut down trading for the remainder of the day.

Under the New York Stock Exchange's rules, the first two circuit breakers stop applying at 3:25 p.m. ET, while the 20% threshold remains active throughout the session.

The rules apply to the entire U.S. equity market, not just stocks included in the S&P 500. A market-wide halt would also suspend trading in companies listed on the Nasdaq and other US exchanges.

The technology sector was particularly hard hit as investors continued digesting the consequences of Tuesday's election results. At around 11 a.m. ET, the Nasdaq Composite had fallen nearly 5%, while the S&P 500 was down 3.2%.

Nvidia lost more than 6%, while the broader semiconductor index plunged nearly 11%. Companies involved in building AI infrastructure were also under pressure as investors worried that Democratic victories could bring stricter oversight of data-center construction.

"We suspect there is going to be some scrutiny on this aggressive AI buildout," Ipek Ozkardeskaya, senior analyst at Swissquote Bank, told Reuters.

The selloff also spilled into cryptocurrencies. Bitcoin fell below $76,000, wiping out its post-election recovery as approximately $428 million in leveraged crypto long positions were liquidated within an hour, as Decrypt reported earlier today.

Stocks also faced pressure from persistent inflation concerns and uncertainty over the Federal Reserve's next interest-rate decision. Investors are awaiting Friday's October employment report, which could provide further clues about the direction of monetary policy.

Despite Thursday's sharp losses, the S&P 500 had not reached its first market-wide circuit breaker at the time of this report.

#5079. Binance, Deribit revoke SignalPlus API keys after fake Teams invite attack

SignalPlus said attackers compromised an engineer’s computer in a breach that resulted in losses of less than $82,000, which the crypto trading platform pledged to reimburse in full.

Exchanges Binance and Deribit said Thursday they had revoked API keys connected to crypto derivatives trading platform SignalPlus as a precaution following a security breach. Both exchanges said their own systems were unaffected, while Deribit warned that trading, monitoring and automation through SignalPlus had stopped working for its customers.

Binance co-founder Yi He also urged third-party platforms integrated with the exchange to review their security controls and monitor for suspicious activity, warning that the threat extended beyond any single company.

SignalPlus provides trading infrastructure for crypto derivatives, particularly options, allowing professional and institutional traders to manage positions across multiple exchanges through a single platform.

According to the company on X, the attack originated from a compromised Telegram account belonging to a contact at a third-party technology vendor. The attacker impersonated the contact and sent a fake Microsoft Teams meeting invitation to a SignalPlus engineer, compromising the employee’s computer.

The attacker remained dormant on the compromised computer before using the engineer’s access privileges to target SignalPlus’s internal network early Wednesday, the company said.

SignalPlus said it detected and contained the intrusion, with total losses of less than $82,000, and had begun fully reimbursing affected users. The company temporarily suspended its services and expects to restore operations over the next few days after completing security reviews and applying patches.

SignalPlus also acknowledged that it had not required IP whitelisting for exchange API access. “We take responsibility for this oversight,” the company said, adding that it would tighten API access requirements and strengthen internal security controls. IP whitelisting is a security measure that restricts API access to a predefined list of approved internet addresses.

Related: BitMart founder says security team ‘severely understaffed’ after new hack

The SignalPlus incident comes days after Binance disclosed details of a $6.3 billion hack on Oct. 29, which also involved compromised employee computers.

According to Binance’s postmortem, attackers posing as recruiters tricked an engineer into downloading a malicious file disguised as a coding assignment. The attackers subsequently used the compromised computer to gain access to additional systems and eventually breach the exchange’s wallet infrastructure.

Binance co-founder Yi He said Tuesday that the exchange had detected and prevented 57 suspected North Korea-linked cyber threats in 2026, including fraudulent job interviews, malicious software packages and attempts to steal employee credentials.

#5076. Reigning Champ Hellen Obiri Didn’t Change Much About This Year’s Prep for the NYC Marathon

Her secret weapon to a successful training block? A lot of naps and even more tea.

Women's Health may earn commission from the links on this page, but we only feature products we believe in. Why Trust Us?

Speaking from personal experience, the closer you get to marathon day, the louder the worries become. Can I really make it to the start line when I’m so sore and tired all the time? Are my long runs and weekly mileage on par with everyone else’s? Do I even want to be doing this anymore? That is, unless you’re the reigning champion, Hellen Obiri.

This weekend, she’ll be making her return to the New York City Marathon—which she won last year in a course record of 2 hours, 19 minutes, 51 seconds. When I chatted with the On athlete a few weeks out from the race, she had just wrapped up a morning session of 12.4 miles and still had another five on deck later that day. It seems like a lot, but “the evening session is always very easy,” she assured me.

Obiri, 36, has won four marathons (NYC and Boston, two times each), and though she knows everyone will be watching to see if she’ll do it again, she doesn’t seem affected by the pressure. “When you're the reigning champion, everybody's looking at you and wondering what you can do,” she says. “You have that pressure of, ‘How can I win back to back?’”

But Obiri knows that in a marathon, anything can happen. It’s a long distance, and she’s competing against the best of the best—so she’s not counting a single person out. “For me, [the key] is to keep training well, and to keep that in my mind: I'm the defending champion,” Obiri says. “The most important thing is to stay safe and to stay healthy, and then everything's possible.”

Here’s what she’s doing to prepare for race day—and her advice to those heading to a starting line soon.

Because, well, it worked so well in 2025. “Sometimes we do study who is in the race,” she admits, though she didn’t name drop any one contender she has her eye on this year.

Each week, she logs over 100 miles, and gives herself two to three days to recover after a long run or speed session. (Recovery days still often consist of easy miles, though.) Her longest run heading into November 1? Twenty-four miles. Even the best of the best don’t run the full distance until race day.

She also preps—both physically and mentally—for pushing through particularly hard moments. Will she still have enough in the tank to keep pushing the pace with her speedy competitors come mile 20? What about in those last 100 meters if she needs to dig deep for a sprint finish?

It’s her second year working with On Athletic Club coach Laura Thweatt, who also boasts an impressive history with the NYC Marathon, placing eighth in 2021. Having a coach who has also raced New York has made a huge difference, Obiri says, because she knows what it’s like to train for this specific race and also what it takes to do well. Plus, Thweatt is able to join her on runs (though she won’t be running the race herself). “She’s my training partner,” Obiri says.

Thweatt is also Obiri’s first-ever female coach, and that’s not to be underestimated, either. “To have a female coach, you can't hide something from her,” Obiri says. “She already knows everything, what you're going through in your mind, in your body.”

Beyond running, Obiri does strength work three times per week, focusing on lower-body muscles like her calves and glutes, but surprisingly, she’s pretty focused on her arms, too. At her level, everything counts, including how strong your arms are when you’re swinging them during a race—an area that needs some improvement, she says.

She naps. Often. After her morning session, Obiri takes a snooze. Then, she heads to lunch. After lunch? She’s napping again. “I sleep a lot,” she says. Obiri estimates that she spends anywhere from five to six hours a day napping.

For Obiri, the basics—sleeping, eating, and hydrating—are the most important things for feeling her best. And when it comes to hydration, Obiri’s not getting caught up in the electrolyte craze or downing gallons of water. Instead, she relies on tea as her main source for fluids. She drinks all different kinds, but will make it in a big batch, complete with milk and three spoonfuls of sugar per cup.

“I’m addicted to tea with sugar,” she says. She has about seven cups per day, including before a run. It’s an easy way to get some extra carbs in, for sure. Along with her tea, before her 6:45 a.m. run, Obiri will have two slices of bread and a banana. “I can’t go on my runs with an empty stomach,” she says.

“The most important thing is to listen to your body,” she says. You might be feeling the excitement right off the bat or trying to keep up with the crowd, but remember to pay attention to how you feel and not go too hard too soon. “Don't go crazy because it's a marathon—it's a long journey—and needs patience,” she says. Make sure you’re keeping on top of fluids and fuel, too.

At this point, you may be deep into training and feeling drained—that’s completely normal, but does require a mindset shift. When Obiri is lacking motivation, she just tells herself she has to do it, or at least start it. “If I start, I will finish it,” she says. And, when a run gets hard? No matter how far she is from the end, she tells herself, “I’m almost there.”

It’s possible your For You Page (like mine) is filled with people freaking out about where they’re at in training—and it can be pretty easy to compare yourself to them. Maybe you have friends crushing long runs on Strava and are starting to wonder if it says anything about your progress.

Whatever it is, stay focused on your own program. “Everybody trains differently, so just know you have trained well and focus on your body, and trust me, you’ll be at the finish line and run well,” Obiri says.

Ultimately, your marathon is about you and what you did to get there. “Trust yourself, trust what you have been training for, and be ready to do what you have been training for six months or longer,” Obiri says.

She reports, writes, and edits content ranging from service stories to in-depth features and awards packages across all verticals, including health, fitness, sports, and beauty. While at WH, she’s traveled to Paris, France to watch Faith Kipyegon attempt a sub-4-minute mile and covered major races like Ultra Trail du Mont Blanc in Chamonix. Olivia is a RRCA-certified running coach who is especially passionate about helping people start their running journey and is training for her second marathon. Her works also appears in New York Magazine’s The Cut, PS (formerly Popsugar), and Salon, where she also did on-camera interviews with celebrity guests.

#5072. Trump-Backed American Bitcoin Turns a Profit as Bitcoin Rise Lifts Books

Trump-Backed American Bitcoin Turns a Profit as Bitcoin Rise Lifts Books

American Bitcoin, the Trump-family-backed mining venture, swung to a roughly $126 million profit in the third quarter, a sharp reversal from the loss it posted three months earlier, as a jump in Bitcoin’s book value and disciplined accumulation powered its best showing yet.

The Nasdaq-listed company, which trades under the ticker ABTC and is majority-owned by miner Hut 8, reported net income of about $126 million for the quarter ended Sept. 30, compared with a $57.2 million net loss in the second quarter.

Adjusted EBITDA came in at roughly $221 million, versus a $45 million loss the prior quarter. Much of the swing came from a $197 million gain on its digital assets, as Bitcoin’s value rose during the period.

The company grew its Bitcoin reserve to about 8,427 BTC, up roughly 425 coins, or 5%, from the end of June, and lifted its “satoshis per share,” a measure of how much Bitcoin backs each share, by about 5%. Notably, it did so without buying any Bitcoin or issuing new shares through its at-the-market program during the quarter, relying instead on its own mining output.

“These are significant results, particularly in a challenging digital asset market,” said Eric Trump, the company’s co-founder and chief strategy officer, touting the profit and the growth in Bitcoin per share. CEO Mike Ho credited “a disciplined, Bitcoin-denominated operating business,” saying the firm continued “mining Bitcoin at a structural discount to spot.”

The mining operation itself had a tougher quarter. American Bitcoin mined about 891 BTC, down from 932 in the prior period, and its cost to mine rose to roughly $42,100 per Bitcoin from $36,500, which it attributed to higher seasonal power costs. Gross margin slipped to 41% from 49%. The company ended the quarter with a fleet of about 89,242 miners and 28.1 exahashes per second of capacity.

The results cap a volatile stretch for the venture. American Bitcoin notched a record mining quarter earlier this year as it crossed 8,000 BTC, but its stock has struggled, hitting new lows and prompting a 1-for-15 reverse stock split in July. Shares have fallen well below their debut price since the company went public through a merger with Gryphon Digital Mining.

The company’s profit, while striking, rests heavily on an accounting gain tied to Bitcoin’s price rather than core mining operations, meaning a downturn in Bitcoin could just as easily swing the figure back into the red.
