Led by Berkus and partner Lauren Buxbaum Gordon, Nate Berkus Associates operates from offices in Chicago, New York, and Los Angeles, and works on residential and commercial projects around the world. From penthouses to lakeside retreats, Berkus and Gordon craft homes that feel luxurious and are designed to evolve over the years.

In a River North condo designed by Nicholas Moriarty Interiors, a Robert Motherwell painting takes center stage in the living area, which features a trio of Boffi sofas. In the adjacent dining space, Moriarty placed a Flos pendant light above a vintage table and chairs.

Every homeowner is different, so Nicholas Moriarty Interiors tailors the design process to each client and the way they think. Each project begins with a deep dive into the client’s lifestyle and story through written and verbal questionnaires and “visual listening activities”, resulting in expertly curated and personality-packed interiors. The multi-disciplinary firm focuses on full-scale interior design and renovation projects and offers everything from interior architectural consulting and space planning to art curation and custom furniture and casegood design. Since it was founded in 2008, Nicholas Moriarty Interiors has become known for its artful, deeply considered designs and counts celebrities, Fortune 500 execs, and artists among its clients and fans.

For a project on Chicago's North Shore, North Shore Nest helped their client make her dark home office something special. Designers Melinda Cahill and Suzanne Glavin coated the wood paneling with high-gloss blue paint and brought in layers of blues, pinks, neutrals, and raspberry tones for an overall light and airy feel.

In 2004, Melinda Cahill and Suzanne Glavin teamed up to found North Shore Nest, their Glenview-based residential interior design firm. The design duo and their team specialize in warm, inviting spaces for families and young professionals, imbuing rooms with color, pattern, and a palpable sense of fun. North Shore Nest offers full-service interior design as well as full-day or hour-long consultations. Clients have a dedicated concierge to keep them informed with frequent updates throughout the design process. The firm works on projects in the Chicago area, as well as with clients in Lake Geneva, Wisconsin, and has recently expanded its reach with new construction projects in Arizona and Florida.

There’s no shortage of storage in this River North kitchen, which Outline Interiors designed with the client's love for cooking and entertaining in mind.

Outline Interiors doesn’t just design homes, it also creates moments of joy for their clients and anyone who enters their spaces. Founder Laura Tribbett earned her stripes at several AD100 firms in New York before launching her Windy City firm in 2015. The studio specializes in modern, tailored, and comfortable style, and isn’t afraid to go bold, whether it’s adding plaid wallpaper to an office or installing dramatic black cabinets in a modern kitchen.

Tribbett and her team collaborate with a trusted and talented roster of architects, contractors, and vendors to execute their vision, and the firm carefully manages each stage of the process–even helping clients shop for a home or start a new build.

In a Glen Ellyn home, Park & Oak paired a deep blue paint color with dark wood detailing to create a cozy, moody feel that's both classic and fresh.

Charming details abound in the work of Christina Samatas and Renee Rice, founders of Glen Ellyn–based Park & Oak. The firm specializes in homes that feel personal and timeless, working with clients across the United States on renovations, new builds, and full-home decorating projects. Park & Oak’s beautifully layered interiors feature natural materials, statement lighting, vintage pieces, and subtle patterns. In addition to the design studio, the company showcases its signature style at its shop, Park & Oak Collected, and at its cocktail lounge, The Parlour.

A primarily monochrome living room by Paula Interiors is accented with yellow throw pillows.

Paula Interiors focuses on helping clients create inviting, beautiful, and functional spaces. For the past 12 years, founder Paula Rodriguez has worked on a variety of projects, which include both residential and commercial properties. No matter the size, style, or complexity of design, Rodriguez’s clientele has always been most impressed by the personal experience that Paula Interiors provides. Rodriguez strongly believes that an interior designer should not have a specific style or look that is signature to their company. Instead, she believes in a style-discovery process, in which the client and designer build a vision collaboratively. Her objective with every project is to transform a space into an innovative room that meets the needs of her clients while still producing a wow factor each time they enter their newly designed space.

Rebel House infused the custom white oak kitchen in a Winnetka new build with luxe details, including antique brass accents, an island with reeded end panels, and a walk-in pantry hidden behind a steel-and-glass door.

Creativity and precision come together in the work of Winnetka-based Rebel House. Led by creative director Marli Jones and managing director Michael Kreuser, the firm specializes in new construction, gut renovations, as well as furnishing and boutique hospitality projects, and is known for its ability to carefully manage projects, keeping them on schedule and budget. Exceptional and eye-catching materials are a throughline in the studio’s projects, which range from sophisticated Gold Coast apartments to modern retreats in Utah and Wisconsin. Rebel House has a collaborative process, enhancing their clients’ visions with their own design prowess.

In a co-op apartment in Chicago’s Gold Coat area, Sarah Montgomery Interiors added character to the living room with wood paneling and a stone fireplace surround. A piece by artist hangs above the fireplace and the wood and suede chairs are by Brazilian architect Carlos Motta.

With a background in set design and as a prop master, designer Sarah Montgomery knows how to set a scene. The interior designer blends her experiences in theater and luxury product design in her interiors, which feature lively colors and patterns, worldly influences, artful accents, and striking lighting. Operating out of a stylish Logan Square studio, Sarah Montgomery Interiors specializes in full-service interior design and renovations, transforming historic homes in Chicago and beyond. Montgomery and her team beautifully preserve a home’s past while updating it to work for the way their client lives today.

In a Chicago home renovated by Sasha Adler Design, the island’s brass legs and custom etageres add jewelry-like details to the space. An oak and glass archway frames the passage into the breakfast room.

Sasha Adler brings a wealth of stylish experience to each project, drawing on her time in the fashion industry and the 14 years she worked as design director for Nate Berkus. Adler and her team specialize in historical restorations and new construction in their work around the globe. From artful Chicago apartments to luxe private planes, the designer enhances each space with a harmonious mix of vintage and modern furnishings and one-of-a-kind details. Sasha Adler Design collaborates closely with clients to make sure each project fits with their lifestyles, goals, and budgets.

Interior by Soucie Horner Design Collective

Bespoke design is not just a nice-to-have for this interiors studio, it's an entire mantra. Each project at Soucie Horner Design Collective is considered individually—and holistically—to craft a solution that feels as unique as the clients, the site, and the particular circumstances themselves.

In the dining room of a Chicago condo, Studio Sven found inexpensive textured pendant lights on Etsy and had them painted a bold plum color, which compliments the 70s art and fuschia chairs.

Homes by Chicago-based Studio Sven are not for shrinking violets or minimalists. The bold, outside-the-box interiors crafted by founder Lauren Svenstrup and her team are a feast for the eyes with surprising color palettes, sculptural furnishings, and plenty of unexpected details. Whether working on new construction, renovations, or full-home furnishings projects, Studio Sven creates a collected atmosphere that’s striking yet approachable and made to work with the way a client lives.

Light and bright was the objective for this kitchen, designed by Studio W Interiors.

Based in Hinsdale, a suburb of Chicago, Studio W Interiors is a full-service residential design firm that works on new construction, renovations, and furnishing projects across the US. From concept to completion, the studio works collaboratively with clients and trades to create beautifully designed homes that aim to transcend trends. The defining philosophy that guides the firm is to create a cohesive home that is elevated and inviting with a focus on quality materials. After studying marketing at Loyola University of Chicago and furthering her education in interior design at the School of the Art Institute, Diana Wagenbach began her own namesake company in 2020. Known for her thoughtful design, she works to create spaces that make life feel calm and inspired. She oversees all design projects to ensure they are a reflection of the client while incorporating the firm’s timeless and organic aesthetic.

Floral patterns aplenty in this dining room by Summer Thornton Design, including the Jaipur-influenced Iksel wallpaper, while real flowers add even more drama.

Summer Thornton Design is known for fearlessly bold and colorful interiors. Headquartered in Chicago, the firm works throughout North America, with current projects including the renovation of a Tuxedo Road estate in Atlanta; a Presidio Heights home renovation; a private club in New York City; a Naples, Florida, vacation home addition; a new build in Mexico; and numerous projects in the Chicago area. Founder Summer Thornton firmly believes that each client’s home should be a reflection of their personal style—their soul, passions, and quirks should be found on the walls and in the furniture throughout the home—so no two projects should ever look alike. With over 100,000 followers on Instagram, her inspiring style has been often imitated, but never replicated.

For this entryway, Suzanne Lovell Inc. combined checkerboard tiles and simple details for a classic look.

Suzanne Lovell Inc. (SLI) is a preeminent architecture and interior design firm that also features a boutique fine art advisory. Since 1985, SLI has created award-winning projects for residential clients in Chicago and around the world, including Manhattan, Miami, Naples, Palm Beach, Captiva Island, San Francisco, Hilton Head Island, and the South of France. An experienced team of professionals well-versed in coordinating all aspects of residential projects, the firm creates couture environments through the integration of interior architecture, sophisticated materials, custom furniture, textiles, antiques, and fine art collections that convey each client’s distinct personality.

Beyond solely interior design, Harvard, Illinois-based The Design Coach fills in gaps in the design process for clients, providing management for maintenance and household services, as well as lifestyle support for both everyday tasks and special events.

Third Coast Interiors revived an original structure built by iconic Chicago architect Benjamin Marshall, incorporating a custom rug from Oscar Isberian Rugs and Interior Crafts sofas upholstered in the firm’s own fabrics.

Led by designer Guinevere Johnson, Chicago’s Third Coast Interiors focuses on balancing livability and aesthetics, resulting in homes that feel comfortable and timeless. Architectural details, striking materials, and one-of-a-kind elements take center stage in the firm’s designs, which range from beach retreats in Florida to townhouses on Chicago’s Gold Coast. The firm works on residential and commercial interiors in Chicago and beyond, relying on a streamlined process to ensure projects stay organized.

For the living room of the HGTV Smart Home 2023, Tiffany Brooks Interiors created a rich look with a dramatic green walls, vintage finds, and a mix of luxe textures.

Celeb-favorite designer Tiffany Brooks creates homes that are truly personalized to the client, whether it’s a Chicago family or an NBA player. The AD100 designer, whose design for superstar Jimmy Butler graced AD’s November 2024 cover, transforms spaces with color and pattern, incorporating unique design elements and accents that elevate each room into an experience. Brooks and her team work on projects of all sizes, from single-room designs to whole-home renovations to new construction, and the firm collaborates with clients in the Chicago area and across the country.

The office of Tom Stringer Design Partners was named one of Chicago’s coolest offices by Crain’s Chicago Business, and it’s easy to see why. The studio serves as an ever-evolving backdrop for interesting things and seamlessly blends comfortable furniture with antiques and touches of home. The result is a tailored workspace that appeals to the designers’ own tastes and those of their high-end residential clients.

Designer Tom Stringer doesn’t have a signature style; instead, he and his team approach each project with a spirit of discovery, bringing out the unique qualities of the client and their home. Elegance is infused in each of the firm’s expertly tailored interiors, whether it’s an easygoing lake house or a refined penthouse. The widely published designer is an inductee to the Merchandise Mart’s Hall of Fame for Interior design and a winner of multiple awards from the American Society of Interior Designers.

An antique marble fireplace and gilt mirror ground the formal living room in the designer Wendy Labrum’s own Chicago home.

After spending two years living in London and traveling through Europe, Wendy Labrum was inspired to launch her eponymous firm in 2007. The designer brings the inspiration and knowledge gathered during her time abroad to her residential interiors, devising timeless spaces that elegantly mix pieces from different time periods and provenances. Based in Chicago and Park City, Utah, Wendy Labrum Interiors works with clients across the country and specializes in restoring historic homes to their original glory and creating custom residences that feel rich with detail. By working closely with clients, Labrum and her team devise functional, inspiring spaces that serve as a beautiful backdrop for everyday life.

#5007. Crypto exchanges tout cold storage ratios after Binance attack

Coinbase says it keeps more than 99% of customer assets offline, while Gate reports 88.26% of its total assets are kept in cold storage.

Crypto exchanges were quick to reassure users following the attack on Binance’s hot wallet infrastructure last week, with several companies disclosing how they divide assets between cold storage and internet-connected hot wallets this week.

Crypto exchange Gate on Thursday launched a “Proof of Security” (PoSEC) framework, reporting that 88.26% of its total assets were in cold storage and 11.74% in hot wallets. It claims its cold storage could cover 104.58% of customer assets even if its hot wallets were completely drained.

“By publicly disclosing the distribution of assets between platform cold storage and hot wallets, PoSEC provides visibility into asset storage structures and their ability to cover potential losses under extreme scenarios,” Gate said. It noted the data is still undergoing independent review by a third-party audit firm.

Threat actors attacked Binance’s hot-wallet infrastructure last week, stealing approximately $6.36 billion, most of it in about 20 minutes, according to TRM Labs. Binance CEO Richard Teng said the “vast majority” of user assets are held in cold wallets, but the company has not provided a precise split.

Other crypto exchanges have been quick to share their cold storage ratios this week.

In a blog post Wednesday, Coinbase said that more than 99% of customer assets are held in offline cold storage and that it continuously rebalances excess assets to cold storage.

“Binance’s reported hot wallet incident has understandably left customers asking how Coinbase protects customer assets from something similar happening here,” it said.

On Tuesday, Upbit, the largest cryptocurrency exchange in South Korea, confirmed it holds 99.17% of its assets in cold wallets, with the remaining 0.83% in hot wallets as of September 2026. These figures are already disclosed to the Financial Supervisory Service.

“This represents the highest cold wallet storage ratio among South Korea’s five major KRW-based cryptocurrency exchanges and is substantially above the regulatory minimum of 80%,” a spokesperson for Upbit parent company Dunamu told Cointelegraph, in response to questions about South Korea’s call for crypto exchanges to increase their cold storage ratios.

“Our aim is to keep our hot wallet holdings at an absolute minimum, with a target of maintaining them below 1% of customer digital assets,” they added.

Related: Fed, OCC check US banks after Binance hack as experts flag security risks

Other major South Korean exchanges have reported lower cold-wallet shares. Financial Supervisory Service figures cited in earlier reporting put Bithumb at 94.76%, Coinone at 85%, Digital X, formerly Korbit, at 82.79%, and GOPAX at 80.54% at the end of September.

OKX’s existing security overview, last updated on Sept. 18, 2026, said it holds more than 95% of client crypto assets in cold wallets and less than 5% in hot wallets.

The reported ratios are not necessarily directly comparable because exchanges use different reporting periods and may calculate cold-storage shares against different asset bases.

Cold storage ratios, however, offer only part of the picture of an exchange’s security.

Offline wallets can still be exposed through compromised signing systems, inadequate access controls or employees approving fraudulent transactions.

Hong Kong’s Securities and Futures Commission warned in an August 2025 circular that such weaknesses could compromise both hot- and cold-wallet infrastructure.

Magazine: Asian crypto exchanges batten down the hatches as hacks surge: Asia Express

Cointelegraph is committed to providing independent, high-quality journalism across the crypto, blockchain, AI, and fintech industries.

All news, reviews, and analyses are produced with full journalistic independence and integrity. For more details on our standards and processes, please read our Editorial Policy.

#5005. This 322-Square-Foot Apartment Gives Classic Style a Contemporary Update

Some gifts are best kept a surprise until the moment they’re handed over to their recipient. That was the case with this small 322-square-foot apartment located in the Vidny Gorod residential complex, about four miles from Moscow, which the owner, Lara, received as a gift from her daughter, Marta, and her son-in-law. “The couple personally oversaw the renovation, keeping the project a secret until the very end,” says interior designer Polina Chumakova, founder of Lagolga Buro, who oversaw the design. “Lara knew nothing about it and didn’t see the space that they were going to give her until it was completed and she first set foot in her new home.”

In the living room, the custom cabinet by Avantiz complements a vintage table and chairs by Tok. On the tabletop, an arrangement of vintage vases by Mak.buro adds a decorative touch.

Lara’s passion for cooking and baking guided some of the design choices, as her daughter envisioned turning the 322-square-foot apartment into a home capable of welcoming the entire family. “It was important to create a comfortable space not only for cooking but also for spending time together, so that on weekends the apartment could be filled with the aroma of homemade desserts,” explains Chumakova. “Lara has two children—Marta and her brother—and two grandchildren, ages 13 and 9. The family often gets together on weekends and at the holidays.”

The apartment’s contemporary-meets-classic design reflects Vidny Gorod’s architecture and its urban context. “The style was carefully defined, choosing solutions suited to both the home’s size and the budget for this project,” says Chumakova. “We invested in high-quality finishes and carefully selected furniture, lighting, and accessories. The idea was to combine a contemporary, understated design with natural walnut accents and a few vintage details. The result is an interior with a classic character, yet without references to any specific era.”

The wall of the living space is painted an olive green by Little Greene that adds depth and warmth. The Synergy Grace sofa bed by Ormatec is paired with the Firmo bedside cabinet by AM.PM; a vintage wall sconce on the left and an Umbrella floor lamp by Artefact on the right complete the scene. On the wall hangs a work by Ksusha Dmitrieva.

There were certainly some challenges. The original floor plan had issues that required a careful reorganization of the space. “The builder had left a rather bulky load-bearing pillar, as well as two separate small bathrooms. It wasn’t possible to move the column, so we decided to integrate it into the layout, concealing it inside a large closet in the entryway,” explains Chumakova. “This allowed us to create a more functional space while making the structure less noticeable. We also combined the two bathrooms. This created a more comfortable home and also provided additional storage.”

The kitchen was custom-designed by Avantiz and includes elegant vintage pendants.

Another challenge was the lack of natural light. “The apartment receives little direct sunlight, so we paid close attention to the choice of colors and finishes,” says Chumakova. “For example, the light, milk-colored porcelain tile, initially selected for the floor, appeared much more beige in the space than we had expected. We replaced it and also adjusted the other colors to account for this effect, striving to keep the interior as bright and warm as possible.” The lighting design also took into consideration the various needs of daily life, without relying on single light sources. “We planned several different lighting scenarios: integrated ceiling lighting, pendant lamps, accent lights for display cases, and other spotlights,” explains Chumakova. “Throughout the apartment, there are about five different types of lighting.”

To make the best use of space, the design focuses on versatile furniture and solutions that serve multiple purposes, helping to avoid clutter in the rooms. “Instead of the classic light fixture above the dining table, we chose a solution that would maintain the open feeling of the space and not limit the table to a specific position,” says Chumakova. “We also opted for a sofa bed instead of a traditional bed. This allowed us to keep more open space and make the room more versatile: During the day, it can be used as a place to relax and spend time with the family.”

In the kitchen, as in the entryway, the floor is covered with white tiles with black square accents, a graphic detail that picks up the colors of the vintage lighting fixtures.

The layout, however, prioritizes the kitchen, designed to be the heart of this home. “We deliberately wanted to dedicate a significant portion of the apartment to it, because it’s one of the most important areas of the home for its owner,” says Chumakova. “The work area is located near the window overlooking a courtyard: It’s a pleasant spot to spend time cooking or simply looking at the scene outside.”

All the storage systems were custom-designed to blend seamlessly with the interiors. “We wanted the cabinets to be almost invisible,” says Chumakova. “There are two large storage units in the apartment: one in the entryway and one in the living room.”

In the living room, custom cabinets by Avantiz are embellished with retro details. The Dovlet House rug, the vintage table, and the chairs combine contemporary pieces with retro charm.

The color choices help define the different areas without visually fragmenting the space. “We used color to distinguish the entryway from the living room, while the ceiling and the doors of the built-in closet in the entryway were painted the same shade,” Chumakova explains. A natural color palette and materials with distinct textures were chosen to create a warm and inviting atmosphere. “The main colors are olive green in the entryway and a milky white in the sofa bed area,” says Chumakova. “More intense touches of orange appear only in certain details, such as the pouf’s upholstery and the floor lamp. Everything complements the natural walnut, one of the key materials in the interior.”

The entryway features walls painted in an olive green from Little Greene paint and a white tile floor dotted with small black accents. A vintage pendant, and the orange Justyna pouf from La Redoute that adds a vibrant pop of color, complete the look.

In the entryway, the floor is covered with white tiles dotted with small black accents, in keeping with the contemporary classic style. The moldings, found in the entryway and on the wall behind the sofa, add rhythm and depth to the surfaces, giving a modern twist to a traditional detail.

The small size of the apartment required some very careful choices when it came to furnishings. “It was important to find furniture on the right scale: pieces that wouldn’t overwhelm the small apartment and that, at the same time, would be consistent with the contemporary classic aesthetic,” says the interior designer. “So we reinterpreted some traditional elements, from walnut moldings to vintage lamps with distinctive shapes, adapting them to a modern residential complex and the compact dimensions of the apartment.”

In the bath, the wooden cabinet is custom-made by Avantiz; the Evana fixtures by Villeroy & Boch and the Oxford faucet by Migliore complement the vintage sconces.

The finished apartment won over Marta, who oversaw every aspect of the renovation along with her husband. “It was a perfectly successful experiment,” the designer says. “Perhaps, if it had been a more conventional project with the future owner, it would have involved them in every phase and it would have been more challenging. It’s possible that some of the bolder solutions might not have been approved.” In this case, the fact that Marta wanted to surprise her mother also provided greater design freedom, resulting in a space that would be perfectly suited to its future resident.

This article originally appeared in AD Italia.
